</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">In US$ million, unless noted otherwise. EBITDA = Operating income + Depreciation of PP&amp;E + Depreciation of the right of
use assets + Amortization of intangible assets + Unproductive exploratory drillings + (Reversal) / Deterioration of PP&amp;E. Adjusted EBITDA = EBITDA that excludes IFRS 16 effects +/- <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">one-off</FONT> items. Net
Leverage Ratio = Net Debt / LTM Adj. EBITDA. FCF = Cash flow from Operations less CAPEX (Investing activities), M&amp;A (Investing activities), and interest and leasing payments (Financing activities). Fuels = diesel + gasoline. R&amp;M is refining
and marketing business, it excludes petrochemicals and agro products. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Adj. EBITDA</B> totaled US$1,245&nbsp;million (+48% q/q), mostly due to higher
prices of fuels and other refined products related to Brent, and OPEX saving from certain mature fields already divested, coupled with the increased value of inventories and processing level of our refineries, ahead of upcoming maintenance stoppage.
Also, 4Q24 was affected by an environmental provision, as indicated in that period. All of them were partially offset by a slightly higher cost of oil purchases to third parties. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">Interannually, adjusted EBITDA was essentially flat, mainly because the higher shale production and local fuel prices, combined with increased processing
level in 1Q25, were offset by a reduced OPEX in 1Q24, impacted by the discrete devaluation of <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">mid-December</FONT> 2023 (partially counterbalanced by lower value of inventories due to the same devaluation). </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Excluding mature fields, 1Q25 proxy adjusted EBITDA would have been US$1,351</B><B></B><B>&nbsp;million</B>. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>CAPEX </B>was US$1,214&nbsp;million, being <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">-8%</FONT> q/q mainly due to higher CAPEX deployed in Downstream during 4Q24
(revamping works in our refineries and seasonality), while +4% y/y primarily boosted by shale expansion. 75% was concentrated on unconventional activities (mostly shale), increasing considerably from 65% in 4Q24. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Shale oil production </B>averaged 147 kbbl/d (+7% q/q and 31% y/y), representing 55% of our total oil production (4Q24: 51% and 1Q24: 44%). <B>Oil
exports</B> reached 36 kbbl/d, being <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">-11%</FONT> q/q to deliver more own oil to La Plata refinery, while +34% y/y driven by shale oil expansion. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Progress on our main projects: </B></P> <P STYLE="font-size:6pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<TABLE STYLE="BORDER-COLLAPSE:COLLAPSE; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt" BORDER="0" CELLPADDING="0" CELLSPACING="0" WIDTH="100%">
<TR style = "page-break-inside:avoid">
<TD WIDTH="1%">&nbsp;</TD>
<TD WIDTH="2%" VALIGN="top" ALIGN="left">&#149;</TD>
<TD WIDTH="1%" VALIGN="top">&nbsp;</TD>
<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <P ALIGN="left" STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt"><B>Mature Fields Exit Program</B><SUP STYLE="font-size:75%; vertical-align:top">1</SUP><B> </B>(50 blocks): in
early April we signed a MoU with the governor of Santa Cruz Province to move forward with the transfer of the 10 conventional blocks that we operate in that province. From a total of 50 blocks: 11 blocks are divested, 23 blocks in the final stage
and 16 blocks in progress. </P></TD></TR></TABLE> <P STYLE="font-size:6pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<TABLE STYLE="BORDER-COLLAPSE:COLLAPSE; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt" BORDER="0" CELLPADDING="0" CELLSPACING="0" WIDTH="100%">
<TR style = "page-break-inside:avoid">
<TD WIDTH="1%">&nbsp;</TD>
<TD WIDTH="2%" VALIGN="top" ALIGN="left">&#149;</TD>
<TD WIDTH="1%" VALIGN="top">&nbsp;</TD>
<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <P ALIGN="left" STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt"><B>VMOS new oil export pipeline</B> (~550 kbbl/d by 1H27, ~US$3&nbsp;billion CAPEX): YPF holds the largest stake
among the initial shippers (27% = 120/445 kbbl/d). Construction started in <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">Jan-25,</FONT> now working on the oil pipeline routes and trench excavation. We are targeting Project Finance (70% debt and 30% equity).
</P></TD></TR></TABLE> <P STYLE="font-size:6pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>

<TR style = "page-break-inside:avoid">
<TD WIDTH="1%">&nbsp;</TD>
<TD WIDTH="2%" VALIGN="top" ALIGN="left">&#149;</TD>
<TD WIDTH="1%" VALIGN="top">&nbsp;</TD>
<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <P ALIGN="left" STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt"><B>Argentina LNG</B>: in early May, the SPV for Argentina LNG 1, Southern Energy, obtained the FID approval for a
<FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">20-year</FONT> bareboat charter agreement for 2.45 MTPA FLNG Hilli Episeyo (COD expected by 2027), and added a second agreement for 3.5 MTPA FLNG MKII (COD expected by 2028), subject to FID approval, estimated in
2H25. YPF owns a 25% equity stake in Southern Energy. On the other hand, YPF signed a MoU with Eni, a strategic partner to develop Argentina LNG 3 (~12 MTPA).
</P></TD></TR></TABLE><DIV STYLE="line-height:8.0pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:2pt;border-bottom:1px solid #000000;width:11%">&nbsp;</DIV>

mature fields capex was ~US$800&nbsp;million. </P></TD></TR></TABLE>

STYLE="white-space:nowrap">off-peak</FONT> seasonal local demand of diesel (especially in retail and agribusiness) and fertilizers, as well as decreased oil export volumes (but higher own oil processed in our refineries), partially offset by

</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">Stock variations include price effects by US$ 5&nbsp;million in 1Q25, (US$47) million for 4Q24, (US$169) million for
1Q24.&#8195; </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>OPEX</B> totaled US$1,744&nbsp;million <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-14%</FONT> q/q), primarily due to savings from mature fields
already divested and an environmental provision during 4Q24, as indicated in that period, plus lower transportation costs in 1Q25, on the back of fuel sales reduction. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Total Other Costs</B> reached US$1,390&nbsp;million <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-4%</FONT> q/q), mostly explained by the unproductive exploratory
drillings recorded in 4Q24, slightly offset by higher depreciation and amortizations. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Purchases</B><B></B><B>&nbsp;&amp; Stock Variations</B> amounted
to US$959&nbsp;million <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-18%</FONT> q/q). <B>Purchases</B> remained stable q/q, as the lower seasonality in natural gas and fertilizers was offset by increased oil prices and oil purchases, particularly to third
parties, coupled with diesel imports due to higher processing levels in our refineries. <B>Stock variations</B> reached a positive US$69&nbsp;million in 1Q25 (compared to a negative US$157&nbsp;million in 4Q24), mainly explained by stock
accumulation of fuels in 1Q25 (compared to stock consumption in 4Q24). Additionally, in 4Q24, we recorded lower value of inventory of oil, driven by reduced prices. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Other operating net results</B> totaled a loss of US$323&nbsp;million <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-42%</FONT> q/q), primarily explained by lower <FONT
STYLE="white-space:nowrap">one-off</FONT> costs related to mature fields<SUP STYLE="font-size:75%; vertical-align:top">3</SUP> (result from changes in fair value of assets held for sale, provision for obsolescence of materials and equipment,
provision for severance indemnities, provision (recovery) for operating optimizations and result from sale of assets). </P><DIV STYLE="line-height:8.0pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:2pt;border-bottom:1px solid #000000;width:11%">&nbsp;</DIV>

</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Financial net results</B> recorded a loss of US$256&nbsp;million (vs. a loss of US$112&nbsp;million in 4Q24), mostly driven
by extraordinary gains from holding financial securities in 4Q24, and lower domestic interest income rates in 1Q25. The <B>income tax </B>recorded a charge of US$27&nbsp;million (vs. a positive amount of US$225&nbsp;million in 4Q24), mainly due to
the income tax charge of our subsidiaries, while in 4Q24 we accrued a positive income tax effective rate, on the back of lower future tax payables recorded during that period. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">Consequently, the <B>net result</B> totaled a loss of US$10&nbsp;million (vs. a loss of US$284&nbsp;million in 4Q24). </P>

structure of YPF. The main changes are: i) &#147;Gas&nbsp;&amp; Power&#148; segment divided into two segments, &#147;LNG&nbsp;&amp; Integrated Gas&#148; and &#147;New Energies&#148;; ii) &#147;Downstream&#148; segment renamed as
&#147;Midstream&nbsp;&amp; Downstream&#148;; iii) Midstream gas business (formerly included in &#147;Gas&nbsp;&amp; Power&#148; segment) reallocated to &#147;Midstream&nbsp;&amp; Downstream&#148; segment. For more information, please refer to the
Relevant Fact mentioned above and Note 6 of our Financial Statements. </I></P>

</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Revenues</B> totaled US$2,067&nbsp;million (+5% q/q), mainly driven by higher sales of natural gas (+21%), on the back of
peak seasonal demand from power plants and recovery of demand from Mega, under maintenance in 4Q24. Moreover, we recorded higher crude oil price (+3%), partially offset by lower natural gas price <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-6%).</FONT> It is
worth noting that total oil volume remained similar as shale oil growth fully offset lower conventional oil. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Lifting cost</B> decreased to US$15.3/BOE
<FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-12%</FONT> q/q), primarily as a result of lower exposure to mature fields (certain blocks divested in 2024), plus outstanding oil productivity in La Angostura Sur block (south hub). <B>Conventional </B>was
US$30.3/BOE <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-7%</FONT> q/q) and <B>unconventional </B>was US$5.2/BOE <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-5%</FONT> q/q). Excluding mature fields, total lifting cost would have been below US$9/BOE. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">Zooming into the lifting cost in our <B>shale core hub</B> blocks (at 100% stake), it recorded US$4.6/BOE, a competitive level despite the sequential increase
(+11% q/q), mainly derived from higher costs and lower production in La Amarga Chica block, expected to return to normal levels in the following months. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Royalties </B>and other taxes averaged US$6.6/BOE <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-2%</FONT> q/q), mostly due to lower natural gas prices, partially
offset by higher gas production and oil prices. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Other costs</B> amounted to US$564&nbsp;million <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-33%</FONT> q/q),
primarily driven by lower costs associated with mature fields mentioned before<SUP STYLE="font-size:75%; vertical-align:top">4</SUP>, and marginally, unproductive exploratory drillings recorded in 4Q24. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">In 4Q24 the Company recognized an<B> impairment charge</B> of PP&amp;E for the gas units in the Northwest Basin. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Adj. EBITDA</B> totaled US$766&nbsp;million (+28% q/q), mainly boosted by peak seasonal demand of natural gas (especially power plants), increased crude
oil prices and lower losses in adjusted EBITDA from mature fields (1Q25: US$106&nbsp;million vs 4Q24: US$153 million), partially offset by reduced price of natural gas. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>CAPEX</B> amounted to US$979&nbsp;million (+11% q/q), mostly destined for drilling and workover activities. In 1Q25 US$914&nbsp;million was allocated to
unconventional assets (vs. US$861&nbsp;million in 4Q24). </P><DIV STYLE="line-height:8.0pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:2pt;border-bottom:1px solid #000000;width:11%">&nbsp;</DIV>
<TABLE STYLE="BORDER-COLLAPSE:COLLAPSE; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt" BORDER="0" CELLPADDING="0" CELLSPACING="0" WIDTH="100%">
<TR style = "page-break-inside:avoid">
<TD WIDTH="4%" VALIGN="top" ALIGN="left"><SUP STYLE="font-size:75%; vertical-align:top">4</SUP>&nbsp;</TD>
<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <P STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman; " ALIGN="left">Please refer to the &#147;Other operating net results&#148; analysis in section 1 of this Report.
</P></TD></TR></TABLE>

<Center><DIV STYLE="width:8.5in" align="left">

 <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">


<IMG SRC="g932212g10a14.jpg" ALT="LOGO">
 </P> <p STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt ; font-size:8pt">&nbsp;</P>
 <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Unconventional oil drilling and workover activities</B> recorded again strong metrics: (# oil wells) </P>
<P STYLE="font-size:12pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P> <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt" ALIGN="center">


<IMG SRC="g932212g10a17.jpg" ALT="LOGO">
 </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">In terms of <B>efficiencies within our unconventional operations</B>, we continued achieving remarkable results, we
averaged 304 meters/day of drilling speed in our <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">core-hub</FONT> blocks, and 235 <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">stages/set-month</FONT> of fracking speed. Regarding drilling speed, we began the year at a lower
level than expected in some wells of Aguada del Cha&ntilde;ar block, but we bounced back during March, reaching the fastest unconventional drilling speed in this same block: 551 meters/day drilled in 10 days for a well of almost 2,600 meters of
lateral length. </P> <P STYLE="font-size:12pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>

</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Crude oil production</B> averaged 270 kbbl/d (flat q/q), once again on the back of a solid growth in shale oil (147 kbbl/d;
+7% q/q), more than offsetting the contraction in conventional output <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-6%</FONT> q/q), including mature fields (also, certain mature fields were divested in 2024). </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Natural gas production </B>grew by +9% q/q, mainly driven by higher seasonal demand from power plants. <B>NGL production</B> increased by +34% q/q,
returning to normal levels after the reactivation of Mega facilities. </P>
 <p STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt ; font-size:8pt">&nbsp;</P> <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman" ALIGN="right">Page 8/16 </P>

</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">Stock variations include price effects by US$ 8&nbsp;million in 1Q25, (US$40) million for 4Q24, US$102&nbsp;million for
1Q24.&#8195; </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:6pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Revenues</B> totaled US$3.9&nbsp;billion <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-3%</FONT> q/q), mainly explained by lower seasonal diesel
demand (especially in retail and agribusiness) and fertilizers, in addition to a decline in oil exports, driven by a higher vertical oil integration with our La Plata refinery, and discontinuation of jet fuel sales in our subsidiary in Chile. These
effects were partially offset by better local fuel prices and growing jet fuel sales in the local market (higher demand and prices). </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>OPEX
Downstream</B> amounted to US$535&nbsp;million <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-7%</FONT> q/q), primarily because of higher maintenances in 4Q24, while in 1Q25 we recorded lower fuel transportation costs, on the back of fuel sales reduction. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Fuel imports</B> reached US$59&nbsp;million (+113% q/q), mostly driven by higher diesel volumes, especially to accumulate stock and prepare for the
upcoming maintenances in 2Q25. As a result, fuel imports remained at low levels, representing only 2% of total fuel sales, compared to 1% in 4Q24 and 4% in 1Q24. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Crude oil purchases</B> (including intersegment) were US$2,131&nbsp;million (+5% q/q), mostly due to higher oil prices and higher volumes purchased from
third parties, in line with increased processing levels, ahead of the upcoming maintenances mentioned above. <B>Biofuel purchases</B> rose 6% q/q, mainly due to a 10% growth in biodiesel (+8% in volume and +2% in price), especially for restocking
purposes, and 4% increase in bioethanol (+5% in volume but <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">-1%</FONT> in price), in line with gasoline production. <B>Agro products purchases </B>declined 11% q/q, primarily in line with lower seasonal sales of
fertilizers mentioned before. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Stock variations</B> totaled a positive US$104&nbsp;million (vs. a negative US$150&nbsp;million in 4Q24), mainly due to
a substantial increase in inventory&#146;s restock in 1Q25, aimed at preparing for the upcoming maintenance shutdown, compared to an inventory&#146;s consumption in 4Q24. This was also coupled with a higher price of oil in 1Q25. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Other costs </B>decreased 25% q/q, mainly due to an environmental provision as indicated in 4Q24, while in 1Q25 we discontinued jet fuel operations in our
subsidiary in Chile. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Adj. EBITDA,</B> excluding inventories price effect of oil products, totaled US$504&nbsp;million (+36% q/q), mainly boosted by
higher prices of local fuels and other refined products linked with better international prices, while in 4Q24 we recorded an environmental provision and increased costs related to higher maintenances, partially offset by lower seasonal diesel
demand. </P>
 <p STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt ; font-size:8pt">&nbsp;</P> <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman" ALIGN="right">Page 9/16 </P>

</DIV></Center>

 <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Adj. EBITDA of the Refining</B><B></B><B>&nbsp;&amp; Marketing</B> business, in unit terms, reached
US$14.3/bbl (+28% q/q), boosted by local fuel price increase and continuous efficiencies gained, such as optimized energy consumption and steam and gas recoveries. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>CAPEX</B> was US$204&nbsp;million <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-42%</FONT> q/q): 53% refining, 22% midstream (O&amp;G), 20% logistics and 5% others.
</P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">In our <B>refineries,</B> CAPEX was allocated on the following main projects: </P> <P STYLE="font-size:12pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>

Energy. In that sense, the construction of a new diesel oil hydrotreatment unit at Luj&aacute;n de Cuyo refinery continued making progress, expecting to be operational in 1H26. </P></TD></TR></TABLE>

<TD NOWRAP VALIGN="bottom">%&nbsp;</TD></TR></TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">Net Average domestic prices for gasoline and diesel are net of
taxes, commissions, commercial bonuses and freights. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Crude oil processed </B>averaged 318 kbbl/d (+5% q/q), recording a strong refinery utilization of
94%, mainly due to a better performance at La Plata Refinery (which was affected by a maintenance stoppage in 4Q24), partially offset by maintenance shutdowns in Plaza Huincul and Luj&aacute;n de Cuyo refineries. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Domestic fuels sales volumes</B> recorded 3,405 km3 <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-5%</FONT> q/q), but below market contraction (slightly gaining
market share), mainly driven by lower seasonal diesel demand <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-7%</FONT> q/q), also explained by the fact that Q1 has fewer days than Q4. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Petrochemicals sales volumes</B> declined 25% q/q, due to lower foreign and local methanol demand. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Fertilizers, grain and flours sales volumes</B> decreased 18% q/q<B>,</B> mostly explained by lower seasonal demand of fertilizers. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Net average fuels prices in the domestic market measured in dollar terms </B>increased 2% q/q, driven by the price adjustments to converge with
international parties. As a result, the gap with import parities remained in positive territory at 1%, compared to 3% in 4Q24 and minus 7% in 1Q24. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Prices for petrochemical</B><B></B><B>&nbsp;&amp; other refined products</B> expanded by 8% q/q, aligned with an upward trend in international prices of
petrochemical and certain refined products, primarily coal, LPG and methanol. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:18pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>3.3 LNG&nbsp;&amp; INTEGRATED GAS </B></P>

<Center><DIV STYLE="width:8.5in" align="left">

 <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">


<IMG SRC="g932212g10a14.jpg" ALT="LOGO">
 </P> <p STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt ; font-size:8pt">&nbsp;</P>
 <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Adj. EBITDA</B> reached a negative US$5&nbsp;million (vs. a negative US$3&nbsp;million in 4Q24), mainly
because the decreased natural gas price, as producer, and lower seasonal residential demand were partially offset by higher seasonal demand from power plants and the recovery of sales to Mega (unavailable in 4Q24). </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">Regarding the main project, we made progress in: </P> <P STYLE="font-size:6pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<TABLE STYLE="BORDER-COLLAPSE:COLLAPSE; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt" BORDER="0" CELLPADDING="0" CELLSPACING="0" WIDTH="100%">
<TR style = "page-break-inside:avoid">
<TD WIDTH="1%">&nbsp;</TD>
<TD WIDTH="2%" VALIGN="top" ALIGN="left">&#149;</TD>
<TD WIDTH="1%" VALIGN="top">&nbsp;</TD>
<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <P ALIGN="left" STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt"><B>Argentina LNG Project: </B>in early May, the SPV for Argentina LNG 1, Southern Energy, obtained the FID
approval for a <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">20-year</FONT> bareboat charter agreement for 2.45 MTPA FLNG Hilli Episeyo (COD expected by 2027), and added a second agreement for 3.5 MTPA FLNG MKII (COD expected by 2028), subject to FID approval,
estimated in 2H25. YPF owns a 25% equity stake in Southern Energy. On the other hand, YPF signed a MoU with Eni, a strategic partner to develop Argentina LNG 3 (~12 MTPA). </P></TD></TR></TABLE>

payments (Financing activities). </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Free cash flow</B> reached a negative US$957&nbsp;million, despite CAPEX was fully compensated by adj. EBITDA, mainly
due to the impact of mature fields: (i) <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">-US$230&nbsp;million.</FONT> Moreover, we disbursed <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">-US$211&nbsp;million</FONT> to M&amp;A activities, primarily for the acquisition of Sierra
Chata, contributions and prepayments to affiliates for <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">-US$102&nbsp;million</FONT> and paid debt service for <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">-US$221&nbsp;million.</FONT> Lastly, working capital variations and other
items further pushed our free cash flow down to negative <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">-US$224&nbsp;million.</FONT> </P>

</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">Average interest rates for AR$ and US$ debt refer to YPF on a stand-alone basis.&#8195; </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">As of March&nbsp;31, 2025, YPF&#146;s <B>consolidated net debt</B> totaled US$8,336&nbsp;million (+US$902&nbsp;million q/q), mostly due to new international
and local issuances, growing gross debt to US$9,566&nbsp;billion, while diminishing cash and short-term investments, as explained above. As a result, our <B>net leverage ratio</B> increased from 1.6x in 4Q24 to 1.8x in 1Q25. We expect leverage to
return to a range between 1.5x to 1.6x after we close the mature fields transaction. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">In terms of <B>financing</B>, during 1Q25 we issued a <FONT
STYLE="white-space:nowrap">9-year</FONT> unsecured international bond for US$1.1&nbsp;billion at a yield of 8.50%. The proceeds were disbursed to: (i)&nbsp;refinance <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">Jul-25</FONT> Notes (US$757 million) through a
cash tender offer, prepaying US$315&nbsp;million in <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">Jan-25,</FONT> and a &#147;make-whole&#148; call option for the balance in <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">Feb-25;</FONT> and (ii)&nbsp;acquire 54.45% of Sierra
Chata shale gas block in Vaca Muerta. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">In the local capital market, we issued two new bonds denominated in &#147;Dollar MEP&#148; for a total of US$200
million: (i)&nbsp;US$140 million: <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">2-year</FONT> tenor at 6.25%; and (ii)&nbsp;US$60 million: <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">6-month</FONT> tenor at 3.5%. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">After 1Q25, we issued in the local market: (i)&nbsp;a US$-linked bond for US$204&nbsp;million, with a <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">15-month</FONT> tenor
at 3.95%; and (ii)&nbsp;a <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">hard-US$</FONT> bond for US$140&nbsp;million, with a <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">2-year</FONT> tenor at 7%. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">Additionally, during 1Q25 YPF&#146;s credit rating was upgraded in two agencies, after the upgrade in sovereign ratings: Moody&#146;s (from Caa3 to Caa1 with
a stable outlook) and S&amp;P (from CCC to B+). </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">Regarding our <B>maturity profile</B>, for the remaining 9 months of 2025, the Company faces around
US$800&nbsp;million of manageable maturities, mostly local: US$440&nbsp;million of short-term trade facilities with local banks; US$180&nbsp;million of export-backed bond amortizations; US$280&nbsp;million of local bonds and promissory notes, from
which US$147&nbsp;million have already been refinanced with the latest local issuance; and US$51&nbsp;million with CAF and </P>

<P STYLE="margin-top:18pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>6. ABOUT YPF </B></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:6pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">YPF is the largest energy company in
Argentina, fully integrated in the oil and gas value chain. Our main businesses are: (i)&nbsp;in the upstream, we produce ~40% and ~30% of the country&#146;s oil and gas, respectively, and we are the largest shale producer in Vaca Muerta, in process
of divestment of conventional mature fields; (ii)&nbsp;in the downstream, we operate 3 refineries (~50% of Argentina&#146;s refining capacity) and lead the local diesel and gasoline sales (market share &gt;55%); and (iii)&nbsp;in gas and power,
Metrogas, our subsidiary, distributes ~25% of the country&#146;s natural gas, while YPF Luz, our affiliate, is the third largest power generation company in Argentina. The Government is the controlling shareholder with a 51% stake, and YPF is listed
in the NYSE and ByMA. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:18pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>7. DISCLAIMER </B></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:6pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">Additional
information about YPF S.A., a sociedad an&oacute;nima organized under the laws of Argentina (the &#147;Company&#148; or &#147;YPF&#148;) can be found in the &#147;Investors&#148; section on the website at www.ypf.com. </P>
